Then, applying any (strictly) increasing function f, we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f(Seq(T)_i) \le f(Seq(\widetilde{T})_i) \text { for all } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}f(Seq(T)i)≤f(Seq(T~)i)for alli∈1,…,l, with strict inequality for at least one index precisely if f is strictly increasing. Summing over all indices gives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi ^{Seq}_f(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^l f(Seq(T)_i) \le \sum \limits _{i=1}^l f(Seq(\widetilde{T})_i) = \Phi ^{Seq}_f(\widetilde{T}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΦfSeq(T)=∑i=1lf(Seq(T)i)≤∑i=1lf(Seq(T~)i)=ΦfSeq(T~), which shows that T (uniquely) minimizes the functional for all (strictly) increasing functions f. Note that the minimum is unique precisely if the inequality is strict, which is the case when f is strictly increasing. This completes the proof for minimization.

First, assume that T uniquely minimizes the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{Seq}_f$$\end{document}ΦfSeq for some increasing function f. We want to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Seq(T)_i < Seq(\widetilde{T})_i \text { for at least one } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} \text { and for all }\widetilde{T} \in \mathcal {T} \setminus \{T\}.$$\end{document}Seq(T)i<Seq(T~)ifor at least onei∈1,…,land for allT~∈T\{T}. Assume that this is not the case, i.e., assume there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{T} \in \mathcal {T} \setminus \{T\}$$\end{document}T^∈T\{T} such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Seq(T)_i \ge Seq(\widehat{T})_i \text { for all } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Seq(T)i≥Seq(T^)ifor alli∈1,…,l. Then, while T might minimize the functional, T cannot minimize the functional uniquely, because, by the first part of the lemma, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{Seq}_f(T) \ge \Phi ^{Seq}_f(\widehat{T})$$\end{document}ΦfSeq(T)≥ΦfSeq(T^). This contradicts the assumption and completes the proof for this part.

For the second assertion, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal {T}$$\end{document}T∈T and assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Seq(T)_i \le Seq(\widetilde{T})_i \text { for all } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Seq(T)i≤Seq(T~)ifor alli∈1,…,l and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Seq(T)_i < Seq(\widetilde{T})_i \text { for at least one } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Seq(T)i<Seq(T~)ifor at least onei∈1,…,l for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{T} \in \mathcal {T} \setminus \{T\}$$\end{document}T~∈T\{T}. Then, applying any (strictly) increasing function f, we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f(Seq(T)_i) \le f(Seq(\widetilde{T})_i) \text { for all } i \in \left\{ 1,\ldots ,l\right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}f(Seq(T)i)≤f(Seq(T~)i)for alli∈1,…,l, with strict inequality for at least one index precisely if f is strictly increasing. Summing over all indices gives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi ^{Seq}_f(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^l f(Seq(T)_i) \le \sum \limits _{i=1}^l f(Seq(\widetilde{T})_i) = \Phi ^{Seq}_f(\widetilde{T}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΦfSeq(T)=∑i=1lf(Seq(T)i)≤∑i=1lf(Seq(T~)i)=ΦfSeq(T~), which shows that T (uniquely) minimizes the functional for all (strictly) increasing functions f. Note that the minimum is unique precisely if the inequality is strict, which is the case when f is strictly increasing. This completes the proof for minimization.

Note that if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T} = \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T=BTn∗ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Seq \in \left\{ \mathcal {B}, \mathcal {N}, \Delta \right\} $$\end{document}Seq∈B,N,Δ, then the sequences have the same length for all considered trees. Moreover, the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{Seq}_f$$\end{document}ΦfSeq corresponds to the respective metaconcept.

Having established this useful lemma, we can now begin our analysis of the three classes of metaconcepts. We start with the balance value metaconcept.

Balance Value Metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB

In the following, we prove one of our main results, namely that the BVM is a binary imbalance index for all strictly increasing functions f. This generalizes existing results in the literature, which were previously proven only for specific functions f, such as the Colless index, the corrected Colless index, and the quadratic Colless index. Additionally, we demonstrate that all imbalance indices induced by strictly increasing and affine functions f are equivalent to the Colless index. Furthermore, we show that the mb-tree (uniquely) minimizes the BVM if f is strictly increasing and (locally strictly) convex. In a second step, we compute the minimum and maximum values of the BVM.

We first investigate the relationship between the BVM \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the Colless index, the corrected Colless index, and the quadratic Colless index by focusing on the specific properties of the function f that induces each of these indices.

Remark 3.5

Let f be an affine function, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(b) = m \cdot b + a$$\end{document}f(b)=m·b+a, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈BTn∗. Recalling that a binary tree with n leaves has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n-1$$\end{document}n-1 inner vertices, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f\left( T\right) = \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T\right) } f(b) = \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T\right) } (m \cdot b + a) = \left( m \cdot \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T\right) } b\right) + (n-1) \cdot a \\ & \quad {\mathop {=}\limits ^{\text {cf. Table }2}} m \cdot C\left( T\right) + (n-1) \cdot a. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΦfBT=∑b∈BTf(b)=∑b∈BT(m·b+a)=m·∑b∈BTb+(n-1)·a=cf. Table2m·CT+(n-1)·a.It follows immediately that the BVM with strictly increasing and affine f (i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m > 0$$\end{document}m>0) is equivalent to the Colless index on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗.

Furthermore, by Remark 3.2, the corrected Colless index is equivalent to the Colless index. Additionally, we note that the functions inducing the Colless index (i.e., the identity function) and the quadratic Colless index (i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{QC}(b) = b^2$$\end{document}fQC(b)=b2) are both strictly increasing. The function inducing the Colless index is affine, whereas the function inducing the quadratic Colless index is strictly convex for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b \ge 0$$\end{document}b≥0.

With this in mind, we now turn our attention to the extremal trees of the BVM.

3.2.1.1 Extremal Trees

We begin by analyzing the trees that maximize, respectively minimize, the BVM for (strictly) increasing functions f.

Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_2 = 0 < 1 = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _2$$\end{document}B(T)2=0<1=BTncat2.Now, together with Lemma 3.4, this implies that the caterpillar (uniquely) maximizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗ for any (strictly) increasing function f.Now, we show that the fb-tree of height h (uniquely) minimizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n = 2^h$$\end{document}n=2h for any (strictly) increasing function f. Recall that we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T) = (0, \ldots , 0)$$\end{document}B(T)=(0,…,0) if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T = T^{fb}_h$$\end{document}T=Thfb. In particular, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1,2, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,2,…,n-1, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {B}\left( T^{fb}_h\right) _i = 0 \le \mathcal {B}(T)_i, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}BThfbi=0≤B(T)i, and if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \ne T^{fb}_h$$\end{document}T≠Thfb, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {B}\left( T^{fb}_h\right) _i = 0 < \mathcal {B}(T)_i \end{aligned}$$\end{document}BThfbi=0<B(T)i for at least one \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1,2, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,2,…,n-1. It now follows from Lemma 3.4 that the fb-tree (uniquely) minimizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB in this case, which completes the proof.By both parts of the proof, the BVM is a binary imbalance index for all strictly increasing functions. This completes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We will show that the caterpillar (uniquely) maximizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗. Specifically, we first show that for all trees \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈BTn∗, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i \le \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i≤BTncati for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} .$$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \le 3$$\end{document}n≤3, there is nothing to show, as there exists only one binary tree with n leaves. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 4$$\end{document}n≥4 be the smallest number of leaves for which there exists a tree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈BTn∗ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i > \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i>BTncati for at least one \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1. By assumption, the statement of the theorem holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1cat. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1 be a tree from which T can be obtained by attaching a cherry to one of its leaves. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_n$$\end{document}Tncat can be obtained in the same way from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1cat. Thus, for both trees, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_{1} = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _1 = 0$$\end{document}B(T)1=BTncat1=0, corresponding to the parent of the attached cherry in each respective tree.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) = (0,1, \ldots , n-2)$$\end{document}BTncat=(0,1,…,n-2) and, consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T^{cat}_{n-1}) = (0,1, \ldots , n-3)$$\end{document}B(Tn-1cat)=(0,1,…,n-3). Moreover, attaching a cherry to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1 to obtain T increases each balance value by at most one. Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_{n-1}\right) _{i-1} +1 \ge \mathcal {B}(T_{n-1})_{i-1} +1 \ge \mathcal {B}(T)_i \text { for } i = 2, \ldots , n-1, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}BTncati=BTn-1cati-1+1≥B(Tn-1)i-1+1≥B(T)ifori=2,…,n-1, contradicting the assumption that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i < \mathcal {B}(T)_i$$\end{document}BTncati<B(T)i for at least one \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1.Next, we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i < \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i<BTncati for at least one i. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \ne T^{cat}_n$$\end{document}T≠Tncat, this follows directly from the fact that T has at least two cherries. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_2 = 0 < 1 = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _2$$\end{document}B(T)2=0<1=BTncat2.Now, together with Lemma 3.4, this implies that the caterpillar (uniquely) maximizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗ for any (strictly) increasing function f.

We will show that the caterpillar (uniquely) maximizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗. Specifically, we first show that for all trees \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈BTn∗, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i \le \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i≤BTncati for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} .$$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \le 3$$\end{document}n≤3, there is nothing to show, as there exists only one binary tree with n leaves. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 4$$\end{document}n≥4 be the smallest number of leaves for which there exists a tree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈BTn∗ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i > \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i>BTncati for at least one \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1. By assumption, the statement of the theorem holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1cat. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1 be a tree from which T can be obtained by attaching a cherry to one of its leaves. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_n$$\end{document}Tncat can be obtained in the same way from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_{n-1}$$\end{document}Tn-1cat. Thus, for both trees, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_{1} = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _1 = 0$$\end{document}B(T)1=BTncat1=0, corresponding to the parent of the attached cherry in each respective tree.

Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) = (0,1, \ldots , n-2)$$\end{document}BTncat=(0,1,…,n-2) and, consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T^{cat}_{n-1}) = (0,1, \ldots , n-3)$$\end{document}B(Tn-1cat)=(0,1,…,n-3). Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i = \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_{n-1}\right) _{i-1} +1 \ge \mathcal {B}(T_{n-1})_{i-1} +1 \ge \mathcal {B}(T)_i \text { for } i = 2, \ldots , n-1, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}BTncati=BTn-1cati-1+1≥B(Tn-1)i-1+1≥B(T)ifori=2,…,n-1, contradicting the assumption that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i < \mathcal {B}(T)_i$$\end{document}BTncati<B(T)i for at least one \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \left\{ 1, \ldots , n-1\right\} $$\end{document}i∈1,…,n-1.

Next, we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}(T)_i < \mathcal {B}\left( T^{cat}_n\right) _i$$\end{document}B(T)i<BTncati for at least one i.
