Proof

Let f be an affine function, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(\delta ) = m \cdot \delta + a$$\end{document}f(δ)=m·δ+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m > 0$$\end{document}m>0. The correctness of the maximum value on both \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}Tn∗ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗, as well as of the minimum value on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗, follows directly from the equivalence to the Sackin index, as stated in Remark 3.29, and Lemma 2.9 for the maximum value, respectively Lemma 2.6 for the minimum value.Let f be strictly increasing and convex, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 2$$\end{document}n≥2.First, for the maximum value, by Proposition 3.30, we need to prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f(T^{cat}_n) = f(n-1) + \sum \limits _{i = 1}^{n-1} f(i)$$\end{document}ΦfΔ(Tncat)=f(n-1)+∑i=1n-1f(i). Note that the two leaves in the unique cherry in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^{cat}_n$$\end{document}Tncat have depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n-1$$\end{document}n-1, and for every smaller depth, there is exactly one leaf of this depth. Hence, the stated maximum value is correct.Second, for the minimum value, again by Proposition 3.30, we must show that the gfb-tree attains the stated minimum value. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n = 2^{h_n-1} + p$$\end{document}n=2hn-1+p, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le p \le 2^{h_n-1}$$\end{document}1≤p≤2hn-1. By Remark 2.7, we know that the gfb-tree can be constructed from the fb-tree of height \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 by attaching p cherries from left to right to its leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1. The fb-tree of height \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2^{h_n-1}$$\end{document}2hn-1 leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 and 0 cherries of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n$$\end{document}hn. For each attached cherry, one leaf of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 is replaced by two leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n$$\end{document}hn. Therefore, after attaching p cherries, there are \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2^{h_n-1} - p$$\end{document}2hn-1-p leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 and 2p leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n$$\end{document}hn. This completes this part of the proof.Let f be any increasing function. By Proposition 3.32, we need to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f\left( T^{star}_n\right) = n \cdot f(1)$$\end{document}ΦfΔTnstar=n·f(1). This holds true because all n leaves of the star tree have depth 1.This completes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

For each attached cherry, one leaf of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n-1$$\end{document}hn-1 is replaced by two leaves of depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_n$$\end{document}hn. Let f be strictly increasing and convex, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 2$$\end{document}n≥2.

First, for the maximum value, by Proposition 3.30, we need to prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f(T^{cat}_n) = f(n-1) + \sum \limits _{i = 1}^{n-1} f(i)$$\end{document}ΦfΔ(Tncat)=f(n-1)+∑i=1n-1f(i). Let f be any increasing function. This holds true because all n leaves of the star tree have depth 1.

Let f be any increasing function. By Proposition 3.32, we need to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f\left( T^{star}_n\right) = n \cdot f(1)$$\end{document}ΦfΔTnstar=n·f(1). This holds true because all n leaves of the star tree have depth 1.

So far, we have thoroughly analyzed the three classes of metaconcepts, focusing on the trees that minimize and maximize them, as well as their minimum and maximum values. In the next section, we shift our attention to two additional properties a metaconcept can have: locality and recursiveness.

Locality and Recursiveness

In this section, we analyze the locality and prove the recursiveness of the metaconcepts, focusing again on the first-order metaconcepts. Unlike previous results, the conclusions of this section can generally not be extended to higher-order metaconcepts, not even to those that are equivalent to a first-order metaconcept. For example, Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 12.2 and Proposition 13 proved that the Colless index is local, but the equivalent corrected Colless index is not. Similarly, the Sackin index is local, whereas the equivalent average leaf depth is not (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 5.4 and Proposition 6.3). Further, the recursions for a first-order metaconcept do not account for additional values and, thus, do not apply to higher-order metaconcepts. However, it is worth noting that the corrected Colless index and the average leaf depth are recursive (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 13.2 and Proposition 6.2).

We begin with the locality. Based on the induced imbalance indices, one might conjecture that the BVM, the CSM, and the LDM are local, since all known imbalance indices induced by the corresponding first-order metaconcepts are local. These include the Colless index (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 12.2), the quadratic Colless index (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 15.3), the Sackin index (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 5.4), and the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{s}$$\end{document}s^-shape statistic (Fischer et al. (2023), Proposition 9.3). In the next proposition, we will show that this conjecture is true for all functions in the case of the BVM and CSM, and for affine functions in the case of the LDM.

Proposition 3.34

The balance value metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the clade size metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN are local for all (not necessarily increasing) functions f.The leaf depth metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f$$\end{document}ΦfΔ is local if and only if f is affine, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(\delta ) = m \cdot \delta + a$$\end{document}f(δ)=m·δ+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R.

The balance value metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the clade size metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN are local for all (not necessarily increasing) functions f.

The balance value metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the clade size metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN are local for all (not necessarily increasing) functions f.

The leaf depth metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f$$\end{document}ΦfΔ is local if and only if f is affine, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(\delta ) = m \cdot \delta + a$$\end{document}f(δ)=m·δ+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R.

The leaf depth metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f$$\end{document}ΦfΔ is local if and only if f is affine, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(\delta ) = m \cdot \delta + a$$\end{document}f(δ)=m·δ+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R.

Before proving this statement, we need a lemma that provides an equivalent condition for a function to be affine.

Lemma 3.35

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f: \mathbb {R}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:R→R be a function. Then, f is affine, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(x) = m \cdot x + a$$\end{document}f(x)=m·x+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R, if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(x+z) - f(y+z) = f(x) - f(y)$$\end{document}f(x+z)-f(y+z)=f(x)-f(y) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x,y,z \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}x,y,z∈R.

The proof of this lemma can be found in Appendix B.

Now, we are in a position to prove the proposition above.

Proof of Proposition 3.34

First, we consider the BVM and the CSM. The proof follows the same reasoning as Fischer et al. (2023) (proof of Proposition 12.2 and Proposition 5.4), where the locality of the Colless index and the Sackin index was established. The only difference is that, in our case, the summands are not merely the balance values or clade sizes but rather their evaluations under the function f. However, this modification does not affect the overall argument, thereby proving that both the BVM and the CSM are local.Now, we establish that the LDM is local if and only if f is an affine function, First, we consider the BVM and the CSM. The proof follows the same reasoning as Fischer et al. (2023) (proof of Proposition 12.2 and Proposition 5.4), where the locality of the Colless index and the Sackin index was established. The only difference is that, in our case, the summands are not merely the balance values or clade sizes but rather their evaluations under the function f. However, this modification does not affect the overall argument, thereby proving that both the BVM and the CSM are local.

First, we consider the BVM and the CSM. The proof follows the same reasoning as Fischer et al. (2023) (proof of Proposition 12.2 and Proposition 5.4), where the locality of the Colless index and the Sackin index was established. The only difference is that, in our case, the summands are not merely the balance values or clade sizes but rather their evaluations under the function f. However, this modification does not affect the overall argument, thereby proving that both the BVM and the CSM are local.

Now, we establish that the LDM is local if and only if f is an affine function, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T' \in \mathcal {T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T′∈Tn∗ obtained from a tree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_v$$\end{document}Tv of T with another subtree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T'_v$$\end{document}Tv′, where both subtrees have the same number of leaves and are rooted in v with depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _{T}(v) = \delta _{T'}(v)$$\end{document}δT(v)=δT′(v). Now, we establish that the LDM is local if and only if f is an affine function, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(\delta ) = m \cdot \delta + a$$\end{document}f(δ)=m·δ+a with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m,a \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m,a∈R.

Consider a tree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T' \in \mathcal {T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T′∈Tn∗ obtained from a tree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal {T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈Tn∗ by replacing a subtree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_v$$\end{document}Tv of T with another subtree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T'_v$$\end{document}Tv′, where both subtrees have the same number of leaves and are rooted in v with depth \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _{T}(v) = \delta _{T'}(v)$$\end{document}δT(v)=δT′(v). Observe that the leaf sets outside these subtrees remain unchanged, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_L(T) \setminus V_L(T_v) = V_L(T') \setminus V_L(T'_v)$$\end{document}VL(T)\VL(Tv)=VL(T′)\VL(Tv′), and for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in V_L(T) \setminus V_L(T_v)$$\end{document}x∈VL(T)\VL(Tv), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _{T}(x) = \delta _{T'}(x)$$\end{document}δT(x)=δT′(x), since modifying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_v$$\end{document}Tv does not change the distance from the root to those leaves. Additionally, for leaves within these subtrees, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _T(x) = \delta _T(v) + \delta _{T_v}(x)$$\end{document}δT(x)=δT(v)+δTv(x) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in V_L(T_v)$$\end{document}x∈VL(Tv) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _{T'}(x) = \delta _{T'}(v) + \delta _{T'_v}(x)$$\end{document}δT′(x)=δT′(v)+δTv′(x) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in V_L(T'_v)$$\end{document}x∈VL(Tv′). Now, we compute the difference \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f}(T) - \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f}(T')&=\quad \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _T(x)) + \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T) \setminus V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _T(x))\\&\quad - \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T'_v)} f(\delta _{T'}(x)) - \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T') \setminus V_L(T'_v)} f(\delta _{T'}(x))\\&=\quad \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _T(v) + \delta _{T_v}(x)) + \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T) \setminus V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _T(x))\\&\quad - \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T'_v)} f(\underbrace{\delta _{T'}(v)}_{= \delta _T(v)} + \delta _{T'_v}(x)) - \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T) \setminus V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _{T}(x))\\&{\mathop {=}\limits ^{\text {Lem. }3.35}} \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T_v)} f(\delta _{T_v}(x)) - \sum \limits _{x \in V_L(T'_v)} f(\delta _{T'_v}(x)) = \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f}(T_v) - \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f}(T'_v) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΦfΔ(T)-ΦfΔ(T′)=∑x∈VL(Tv)f(δT(x))+∑x∈VL(T)\VL(Tv)f(δT(x))-∑x∈VL(Tv′)f(δT′(x))-∑x∈VL(T′)\VL(Tv′)f(δT′(x))=∑x∈VL(Tv)f(δT(v)+δTv(x))+∑x∈VL(T)\VL(Tv)f(δT(x))-∑x∈VL(Tv′)f(δT′(v)⏟=δT(v)+δTv′(x))-∑x∈VL(T)\VL(Tv)f(δT(x))=Lem.3.35∑x∈VL(Tv)f(δTv(x))-∑x∈VL(Tv′)f(δTv′(x))=ΦfΔ(Tv)-ΦfΔ(Tv′) Thus, the LDM is local if and only if f is affine, which completes the proof.

A direct consequence of the locality of the metaconcepts is that every subtree of a tree minimizing (respectively, maximizing) a metaconcept, is itself a minimizing (respectively, maximizing) tree for the metaconcept.

Next, we establish the recursiveness of our metaconcepts.

Proposition 3.36

The balance value metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB is a binary recursive tree shape statistic for all functions f. Similarly, the clade size metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN is a recursive tree shape statistic for all functions f. In contrast, the leaf depth metaconcept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_f$$\end{document}ΦfΔ is a recursive tree shape statistic if f is an affine function with an intercept of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a = 0$$\end{document}a=0.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^b \in \mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}Tb∈BTn∗ be a binary tree with standard decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^b = \left( T^b_1, T^b_2\right) $$\end{document}Tb=T1b,T2b such that the maximal pending subtree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^b_i$$\end{document}Tib has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n^b_i$$\end{document}nib leaves. Similarly, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \in \mathcal {T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}T∈Tn∗ be an arbitrary tree with standard decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T = (T_1, \ldots , T_k)$$\end{document}T=(T1,…,Tk), where each maximal pending subtree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_i$$\end{document}Ti has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n_i$$\end{document}ni leaves.

Let f be an arbitrary function, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_m$$\end{document}fm be an affine function with slope \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}m∈R and intercept \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a = 0$$\end{document}a=0. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n = 1$$\end{document}n=1, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b\right) = 0$$\end{document}ΦfBTb=0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T) = 0$$\end{document}ΦfN(T)=0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T) = f_m(0) = 0$$\end{document}ΦmΔ(T)=fm(0)=0. Moreover, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 2$$\end{document}n≥2, we have:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b\right) = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) $$\end{document}ΦfBTb=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2b,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T_i) + f(n_1 + \ldots + n_k)$$\end{document}ΦfN(T)=∑i=1kΦfN(Ti)+f(n1+…+nk),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T_i) + (n_1 + \ldots + n_k) \cdot m$$\end{document}ΦmΔ(T)=∑i=1kΦmΔ(Ti)+(n1+…+nk)·m.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b\right) = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) $$\end{document}ΦfBTb=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2b,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b\right) = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) $$\end{document}ΦfBTb=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2b,

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T_i) + f(n_1 + \ldots + n_k)$$\end{document}ΦfN(T)=∑i=1kΦfN(Ti)+f(n1+…+nk),

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}(T_i) + f(n_1 + \ldots + n_k)$$\end{document}ΦfN(T)=∑i=1kΦfN(Ti)+f(n1+…+nk),

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T_i) + (n_1 + \ldots + n_k) \cdot m$$\end{document}ΦmΔ(T)=∑i=1kΦmΔ(Ti)+(n1+…+nk)·m.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T) = \sum \limits _{i=1}^{k} \Phi ^{\Delta }_{f_m}(T_i) + (n_1 + \ldots + n_k) \cdot m$$\end{document}ΦmΔ(T)=∑i=1kΦmΔ(Ti)+(n1+…+nk)·m.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T = (T_1, \ldots , T_k)$$\end{document}T=(T1,…,Tk), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T^b = \left( T^b_1,T^b_2\right) $$\end{document}Tb=T1b,T2b, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n_i$$\end{document}ni, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n^b_i$$\end{document}nib, f, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_m$$\end{document}fm as described.

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 2$$\end{document}n≥2, we can calculate the BVM as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b\right)&= \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T^b\right) } f(b) {\mathop {=}\limits ^{\text {Rem. } 3.1}} \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T^b_1\right) } f(b) + \sum \limits _{b \in \mathcal {B}\left( T^b_2\right) } f(b) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) \\&= \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ΦfBTb=∑b∈BTbf(b)=Rem.3.1∑b∈BT1bf(b)+∑b∈BT2bf(b)+fn1b-n2b=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2b.Thus, it is a binary recursive tree shape statistic of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x = 2$$\end{document}x=2, where the recursions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_1$$\end{document}r1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_2$$\end{document}r2 are:BVM: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^{cat}_1\right) = 0$$\end{document}λ1=ΦfBT1cat=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&r_1\left( \left( r_1\left( T^b_1\right) \right) ,r_2\left( T^b_1\right) \right) , \left( r_1\left( T^b_2\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_2\right) \right) \\&\quad = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}r1r1T1b,r2T1b,r1T2b,r2T2b=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2bleaf number: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 1$$\end{document}λ2=1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_2\left( \left( r_1\left( T^b_1\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_1\right) \right) , \left( r_1\left( T^b_2\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_2\right) \right) \right) = n^b_1 + n^b_2$$\end{document}r2r1T1b,r2T1b,r1T2b,r2T2b=n1b+n2bHence, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \in \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}λ∈R2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i: \mathbb {R}^2 \times \mathbb {R}^2 \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}ri:R2×R2→R. Moreover, all recursions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_i$$\end{document}ri are symmetric. This completes the proof for the BVM.

BVM: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^{cat}_1\right) = 0$$\end{document}λ1=ΦfBT1cat=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&r_1\left( \left( r_1\left( T^b_1\right) \right) ,r_2\left( T^b_1\right) \right) , \left( r_1\left( T^b_2\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_2\right) \right) \\&\quad = \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_1\right) + \Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_{f}\left( T^b_2\right) + f\left( \left| n^b_1 - n^b_2\right| \right) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}r1r1T1b,r2T1b,r1T2b,r2T2b=ΦfBT1b+ΦfBT2b+fn1b-n2b

leaf number: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 1$$\end{document}λ2=1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_2\left( \left( r_1\left( T^b_1\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_1\right) \right) , \left( r_1\left( T^b_2\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_2\right) \right) \right) = n^b_1 + n^b_2$$\end{document}r2r1T1b,r2T1b,r1T2b,r2T2b=n1b+n2b

leaf number: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 1$$\end{document}λ2=1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_2\left( \left( r_1\left( T^b_1\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_1\right) \right) , \left( r_1\left( T^b_2\right) ,r_2\left( T^b_2\right) \right) \right) = n^b_1 + n^b_2$$\end{document}r2r1T1b,r2T1b,r1T2b,r2T2b=n1b+n2b

This completes the proof for the CSM.

CSM: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}\left( T^{cat}_1\right) = 0$$\end{document}λ1=ΦfNT1cat=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&r_1(\left( r_1\left( T_1\right) , r_2\left( T_1\right) \right) , \ldots , (r_1\left( T_k\right) , r_2 \left( T_k\right) )) \\&= \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}\left( T_1\right) + \ldots + \Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_{f}\left( T_k\right) + f\left( n_1 + \ldots + n_k\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}r1(r1T1,r2T1,…,(r1Tk,r2Tk))=ΦfNT1+…+ΦfNTk+fn1+…+nk,

Discussion

While tree balance is typically quantified using a single index function, Cleary et al. (2025) recently introduced a functional instead of a function to measure tree balance for rooted trees. This functional is based on the clade size sequence of a tree and depends on another function f. In this manuscript, we have generalized this concept to a broader framework, which we call the imbalance index metaconcept of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω. This metaconcept allows for any tree shape sequence as its underlying sequence. Exploiting this property, we introduced two additional subclasses alongside the clade size metaconcept (CSM): the balance value metaconcept (BVM) and the leaf depth metaconcept (LDM). We thoroughly analyzed these three metaconcepts with respect to their underlying function f. As a result, we identified many families of functions f for which these metaconcepts yield (binary) imbalance indices, leading to a range of new imbalance indices. To help users identify a suitable imbalance index obtained from a metaconcept, we provided four decision trees. Additionally, we included R code for computing the metaconcepts and, consequently, the resulting imbalance indices. Furthermore, we analyzed the trees that maximize the metaconcepts for all leaf numbers, as well as the minimizing trees. Finally, we determined their minimum and maximum values.Fig. 9This figure shows (solid line) the strictly increasing and affine identity function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(x) = x$$\end{document}f(x)=x, on which the Colless index and the Sackin index are based. In this case, the BVM \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the CSM \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN have several minima, including the gfb-tree and the mb-tree. Note that all functions of the type \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f(x) = x + \frac{1}{(x+1)^a}$$\end{document}f(x)=x+1(x+1)a for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a \ge 1$$\end{document}a≥1 are such approximations, and all of them will lead to the BVM \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {B}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfB and the CSM \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi ^{\mathcal {N}}_f$$\end{document}ΦfN having the mb-tree as their unique minimum. Also note that we used \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x+1$$\end{document}x+1 in the last case, because using x in the function would not lead to a strictly increasing f for all passed values greater equal zero

Recall that the Sackin index and the Colless index are induced by the first-order CSM and BVM, respectively, when f is the identity function, i.e., a strictly increasing and affine function. Consequently, both are minimized by several trees in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{B}\mathcal{T}^{*}_n$$\end{document}BTn∗, including the gfb-tree and the mb-tree. We proved that when the identity function is approximated from above by a strictly increasing and strictly convex function, the mb-tree becomes the unique minimizer of both the CSM and the BVM. Conversely, when the identity function is approximated from below by a strictly increasing and strictly concave function, the gfb-tree is the unique minimizer of the CSM. Moreover, this result extends not only to the identity function but to all strictly increasing and affine functions. This is remarkable, because minor changes in the function f lead to major changes in the set of minimizing trees. We elaborate on this observation in Figure 9.

Further, since many known imbalance indices also depend on one of the three sequences underlying the metaconcepts, we can classify these indices by determining which metaconcept they satisfy. We found that seven known imbalance indices fall into one of the three metaconcept classes, which can be further divided into several subclasses depending on the choice of f. Consequently, some of our results for the metaconcepts recover results from the literature in a more concise way. For example, Theorem 3.6 unifies six separate proofs from the literature into a single result. Additionally, one subclass, the LDM with strictly increasing and convex f, is totally new to the literature in the sense that none of the existing imbalance indices is induced by it.

A promising direction for future research is to further compare the introduced metaconcepts, for example, by analyzing the resolution of the imbalance indices they induce. It stands to reason that the LDM is the least resolved metaconcept, as for trees with eight leaves, there exist four pairs of trees that share the same \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ (leaf depth sequence), as well as a quartet of trees with identical \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta $$\end{document}Δ values, none of which share the same \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N (clade size sequence) or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}$$\end{document}B (balance value sequence).

Another avenue for future work is to modify the imbalance index metaconcept itself. For instance, one could use sequences based on pairs of vertices (as in the original definition of the total cophenetic index), or consider sequences based on the edges of a tree rather than the vertices.
